# The Corpo-Rat

Journal: `quests/main_quest/prologue/q000_corpo` · `MainQuest` · hash `2305237100`.

Journal premise: So to recap – Abernathy is Jenkins' boss, Jenkins is your boss and he ordered you to off Abernathy. It's a classic damned if you do, damned if you don't situation. You kill her – you're fucked. You tell Abernathy – you're even more fucked. Why? Because for the past few years you've been batting for Jenkins' team. If you get the job done, and do it well, you might get a promotion, but forget sleeping at night, because who knows how soon you'll be in someone's crosshairs. Only proves the wide variety of opportunities Arasaka has to offer.

**Coverage: journal-linked.** 96 exact journal-linked nodes in 12 resources; 14 resources in the inspected dependency context.

Resource roles in that context: 13 content candidate, 1 repair. Role labels describe path/name evidence, not proof of runtime reachability.

Root quest resources with additional journal references: none indexed. Roots remain activation context when a phase/scene anchor exists, to avoid importing sibling launch scaffolding.

Raw direct references, actor bindings, choices, event locstrings and community entries remain in the evidence shards.

## Required acceptance cases before reuse

Exercise normal completion and each named alternate outcome; interrupt and return at every scene handoff; start with spatial/item/actor predicates already satisfied; save/load across waits and joins; cancel parallel listeners; verify reward delivery once; and verify cleanup without changing the committed outcome. Apply the block-specific cases in the [expanded contracts](../all-quest-blocks.md). No installed playtest is claimed.

## Connected paths and supporting bindings

1453 repeated typed/socket path signatures in this selected graph context; 303 distinct direct resource identities; 16 embedded graph handoffs. The full occurrence lists, concrete parameters, exact source pointers and external edges are retained in the checked evidence. Shared forward dependencies can serve several quests; these are context counts.
